- **Authors**: Geonmo Yang, Younggun Cho
- **Comment**: 13 pages. (c) 2024 IEEE. Personal use of this material is permitted. Permission from IEEE must be obtained for all other uses, in any current or future media, including reprinting/republishing this material for advertising or promotional purposes, creating new collective works, for resale or redistribution to servers or lists, or reuse of any copyrighted component of this work in other works
- **Journal**: IEEE Sensors Journal, vol. 25, no. 1, pp. 858-870, 2025
- **Summary**: Underwater visual systems often suffer from blurry textures and low color contrast due to the inevitable light propagation. These issues can significantly degrade the perception of stable robotic operations. In this paper, we introduce a novel learning-based sensing system that tackles the multidimensional vision tasks in underwater; concretely, we deal with image enhancement, depth estimation, and uncertainty for 3-D visual systems. Also, we propose a TRIDENT model in a fast and lightweight manner; TRIDENT consists of three parallelized decoders and one backbone structure for efficient feature sharing. In addition, it is designed to be trained to express complex parameterization. In experimental evaluation on several standard datasets, we demonstrate that TRIDENT significantly outperforms other existing methods on image enhancement and depth estimation. Despite performing three tasks, our model has better efficiency than the others for both memory size and inference time. Finally, our joint learning approach demonstrates robustness in feature matching and seamlessly extends from 2-D to 3-D vision tasks. Supplements are available at https://sites.google.com/view/underwater-trident/home



### What Does Weight-Space Programming Add to Diffusion Editing? Program Construction versus Score-Guided Refinement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22228v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22228v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 05:18:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 05:18:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Zitian Gao, Jingyi Xu, Ziqi Han, Chengkai Lei, Ye Liu, Liangliang Shi
- **Comment**: 25 pages, 7 figures, 13 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Weight-space programming (WSP) lets a language model construct executable weight updates for another network. We study diffusion concept editing through an interface in which programs define training contexts, preservation constraints and update directions, while a shared numerical fitter selects their coefficients. We compare generated programs with published editors, a shared-panel hand-written baseline and budget-matched search over conventional edits on 24 TIMED test edits for Stable Diffusion 1.4. The preregistered rewrite pipeline shows no advantage over the strong baseline. In an exploratory comparison, one-shot Astra programs improve fresh-scene VLM joint success, which requires subject presence together with whole-image target matching, by +10.0 points over the shared-panel baseline, while CLIP relative match shows no detectable change. These programs organise adjustable directions around protected text-state subspaces. Probing and rewriting the Astra programs then raises CLIP by +6.2 points while joint success changes by -9.7. On a fixed 144-image sample, five additional evaluators give point estimates in the same directions under whole-image target matching; checks of subject-local change give less decisive differences. Together, these results identify useful behaviour in complete generated programs and show that score-guided revision need not preserve it: program construction and revision should be evaluated as distinct stages of a WSP pipeline.



### CoSphere-SLAM: Multi-Agent Panoramic Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Spherical Loop Closure
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22235v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22235v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 05:37:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 05:37:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Qi Shao, Zhihao Cao, Shuhao Zhai, Taoyu Wu, Zhuoxiao Li, Anh Nguyen, Long Hu, Xianzhi Li, Hong Liang, Baoru Huang, Liang Li
- **Comment**: 9 pages, 4 figures, 7 tables. Project website: https://cosphere-slam.github.io
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Dense collaborative mapping is essential for search and rescue, building inspection, and warehouse automation, where panoramic cameras capture the entire surroundings in a single frame. Unlike pinhole agents that share views only at similar headings, panoramic agents observe overlapping scenes at any 3D orientation, yet searching full rotations also produces mutually consistent false loop closures across distinct symmetric rooms. Moreover, continuous monocular scale drift splits rigidly merged maps at revisited surfaces, while depth noise at short baselines distorts loop translation directions. To address these challenges, we propose CoSphere-SLAM, the first multi-agent Gaussian splatting SLAM framework for panoramic cameras, whose spherical loop closure aligns spherical features over $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ via fast Fourier correlation across arbitrary orientations and rejects symmetric false closures through global $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ consensus. Building on these closures, directional global optimization solves a per-keyframe $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ pose graph with log-scale regularization to correct scale drift while separating optical-flow translation directions from depth-based magnitudes. Finally, omnidirectional Gaussian fusion transforms each Gaussian with its keyframe similarity and renders Gaussians at their maximum ray response to jointly refine poses and maps. Experiments on indoor and cross-session multi-agent panoramic benchmarks demonstrate that CoSphere-SLAM achieves state-of-the-art trajectory accuracy and rendering quality among single-agent and multi-agent methods. Project website: https://cosphere-slam.github.io.



### Cross3DGS: Unified Vulkan-Based 3D Gaussian Splatting Training from Workstations to Mobile Devices
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22240v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22240v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 05:39:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 05:39:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Hiroyuki Sato, Yusuke Takimoto
- **Comment**: Accepted to the WACV 2027 Workshop on Eyes of the Future (EOF). 19 pages including appendix
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Scenes captured with handheld and wearable cameras are still shipped to a CUDA workstation for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) training. Training on the device that captured them, or on its companion phone or laptop, requires more than translating kernels: GPUs and drivers differ in parallel features, memory limits, and command submission, and these differences decide whether a training schedule executes and returns the correct update. We present Cross3DGS, a cross-vendor, cross-platform Vulkan trainer that keeps one optimizer and chooses its execution schedule per device in two steps: rules on device limits, workload size, and observed driver failures exclude the schedules known not to run correctly, and a fixed preference order selects one of the remaining feasible schedules. The same core completes standard-quality training (30k updates, one million Gaussians) on every configuration we measured, spanning NVIDIA, Intel, Arm, Qualcomm, AMD, and Apple GPUs and including Android devices and an iPhone, whereas the existing portable trainers VkSplat and Brush do not run on some of the mobile GPUs. Averaged over 11 scenes on a workstation, Cross3DGS has higher quality than VkSplat with no significant difference in training time, and Brush reports about three times the training time at comparable quality. The final quality of Cross3DGS is nearly identical across devices. A mobile application turns a handheld capture into a viewable model entirely on the device.



### PhyVGGT: Feed-Forward Articulated Object Reconstruction from Monocular Videos
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22244v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22244v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 05:46:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 05:46:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Qi Shao, Zhihao Cao, Shuhao Zhai, Anh Nguyen, Hong Liang, Baoru Huang, Liang Li
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures, 7 tables. Project page: https://phyvggt.github.io
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reconstructing an articulated object with its 3D movable parts, joint parameters and unseen articulation states from an uncalibrated monocular video is essential for robot manipulation and interactive digital twins. Existing per-scene optimisation methods require minutes to hours and external depth or camera poses, whereas feed-forward 3D foundation models reconstruct dense point maps in one pass without segmenting movable parts or inferring joints. Even on these monocular point maps, regressing or fitting continuous joints directly fails on casual videos, since cross-frame attention blurs moving part boundaries in deep layers, underestimates motion along the viewing ray and leaves continuous axes ill-conditioned under small opening angles. To address these challenges, we propose PhyVGGT, a single-pass framework on a frozen 3D foundation model that anchors moving-part matching and closed-form joint estimation to the undistorted geometry of the stationary base. First, our Part and Motion Decoder fuses intermediate per-frame tokens with global tokens and references part queries to a static-base query to segment 3D parts, align point maps on the static base and predict learned Bingham priors. Second, our Kinematic Inference Layer fits an orthogonal Manhattan frame to static surface normals, selects the joint axis in closed form under a Bingham posterior combining pairwise motion scatter with the learned priors, and solves a linear system for the hinge pivot to drive unseen states. Trained solely on synthetic data, PhyVGGT achieves state-of-the-art monocular performance across five benchmarks, outperforming FAMOS and monocular ArtiPoint, surpassing posed RGB-D ArtiPoint in median error, and running in 6.8 s per 48-frame video against hours of per-scene optimisation in Articulat3D. Project page: https://phyvggt.github.io.



### Multispectral-Polarization-Infrared Multi-Aperture Camouflaged Vehicle Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22246v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22246v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 05:47:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 05:47:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Shu Wang, Xiong Lin, Yuan Song, Junjie Wang, Feng Huang, Wenxi Liu
- **Comment**: 14 pages, 10 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Camouflaged vehicle detection is challenging because camouflage suppresses discriminative cues across sensing modalities. Existing multimodal methods often require precise pixel-level registration and feature-level fusion, creating a trade-off between accuracy and latency. We propose a spectral-polarization-infrared tri-modal detection framework co-designed with a multi-aperture imaging setup to exploit complementary imaging physics. A consensus-guided decision-level fusion strategy combines cross-modal detections within bounded registration tolerances, reducing dependence on precise alignment while maintaining accuracy and real-time performance. We construct a tri-modal camouflaged vehicle dataset containing infrared, polarization, and spectral data. Experiments show that the proposed method achieves 82.7% mean Average Precision (mAP) with an end-to-end latency of 16 ms, outperforming representative feature-level fusion approaches. On the public MID-1K dataset, the framework generalizes to RGB-Depth-Infrared modalities without architectural modification and improves mAP@0.5 by 5.7% over state-of-the-art methods. These results demonstrate the potential of decision-level consensus fusion and multi-aperture sensing for robust and deployable camouflaged vehicle detection.



### SARVIA: Fast-Adapting the Strong SAR Foundation Models That Are Already in Your MLLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22273v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22273v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 06:19:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 06:19:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Li, Zichen Tian, Qianru Sun
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) foundation models provide transferable representations for diverse perception tasks in all-weather Earth observation. However, their development often relies on costly large-scale SAR pretraining, raising the question of whether such extensive domain-specific pretraining is necessary. We find that modern multimodal large language models (MLLMs) already provide visual encoders that can outperform dedicated SAR foundation models as downstream initializations. This motivates a new paradigm: reuse general visual capabilities and devote domain training to brief specialization. We therefore introduce SARVIA, an adaptive post-pretraining method that specializes modern MLLMs using self-supervised visual questions with computable answers. These questions are constructed from unlabeled observations through general image tasks that capture spatial relations and SAR-specific tasks that exploit native measurements and SAR--optical correspondence. A shared answer-prediction objective jointly trains low-rank adapters across visual and language components, with loss-driven task sampling. We then export the adapted visual encoder as a standalone backbone. With 600 adaptation steps and 2 GPU-hours, SARVIA outperforms the compared foundation models on all six SAR detection and segmentation benchmarks. Compared with dedicated SAR pretraining, SARVIA reduces domain-training costs by orders of magnitude while improving visual transfer across architectures and scales. This new paradigm shifts the emphasis from repeated, costly pretraining to a reusable specialization process that can benefit from advances in open MLLMs. Dataset and code: https://github.com/zcablii/SARVIA.



### Heave-Ho: Decentralized Long-Horizon Multi-Humanoid Collaboration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22275v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22275v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 06:21:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 06:46:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Ting-Hsuan Chen, Siheng Zhao, Han Jiang, Qi Pan, Louison Lu, Jiaheng Luo, Henghui Bao, Yiyu Chen, Quan Nguyen, Yue Wang
- **Comment**: 18 pages, 10 figures, 7 tables. Project page: https://heaveho-humanoid.github.io/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Collaboration promises to extend humanoid capabilities beyond what individual robots can achieve. However, realizing this potential requires coordinated whole-body interactions, coherent long-horizon planning, and precise synchronization across robots. We introduce Heave-Ho, a decentralized framework for long-horizon collaborative multi-humanoid loco-manipulation. At the low level, we develop a multi-agent post-training framework that adapts a humanoid foundation tracking model to physically coupled interactions between humanoids, enabling coordinated decentralized control from local observations alone. At the high level, we train a vision-language planner and design a speech-based leader-follower coordination mechanism to synchronize decentralized execution without network communication. Trained with two identical humanoids, our system scales to teams of up to ten robots and generalizes to heterogeneous teams without retraining. To benchmark these capabilities, we introduce HH-Bench, comprising 12 tasks involving teams of 2 to 10 humanoids, including heterogeneous teams. Systematic evaluations show that Heave-Ho consistently outperforms baseline methods, while real-world experiments demonstrate robust decentralized collaboration across challenging tasks.



### Decoding Without a Decoder: Direct Pixel Prediction from Diffusion Transformers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22287v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22287v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 06:40:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:13:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Hansheng Chen, Shengqu Cai, Jan Ackermann, Rayhan Zirvi, Gordon Wetzstein, Xinchen Yan, Leonidas Guibas
- **Comment**: Project page: https://hanshengchen.com/dwd
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Latent diffusion models rely on a decoder to map denoised latents to pixels, adding complexity and inference cost. We introduce Decoding Without a Decoder (DWD), which lets a finetuned diffusion transformer produce final pixels directly through a linear head. Unlike previous methods, where the denoising output leads to final pixels, DWD separates the two roles: the original denoising head continues to drive sampling, then the new DWD head predicts the pixels at the final step. We extend pretrained latent models with DWD efficiently by finetuning only the last few transformer blocks, training the DWD head with pixel quality losses while anchoring the denoising head to the pretrained model to preserve the sample trajectory. Across latent image and video generators, DWD removes the decoder while maintaining or even improving pixel quality. On Wan video generation, it visibly reduces detail distortion and flickering, lowering warping error by 23%. Remarkably, the same separation also improves pixel diffusion models that do not even use a decoder: a final-step DWD head produces substantially better texture detail in text-to-image generation, and improves ImageNet FID from 1.55 to 1.43, a new state of the art among pixel flow models.



### Perception into Imagination: Executable Memory for World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22294v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22294v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 06:52:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 06:52:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Xianyao Li, Ruitong Tian, Rui Min, Fang Xu, Jing Du
- **Comment**: 18 pages, 6 figures, 15 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Physical agents must use what they previously saw or touched after visibility or contact is lost. Their actions change the implications of these remembered facts. We introduce executable perceptual memory (EPM), a world-model framework that advances explicit perceptual state alongside neural latent dynamics during imagination. Its evolving read-out informs reward prediction, value estimation and policy learning. We align reward inputs with the transitions that produced them and supervise the reward head on open-loop rollouts of recorded actions. On Memory Maze $13\times13$, EPM reaches 30.37 return at 1M interactions, compared with 23.37 when the same evolving read-out informs only policy and value on a common perceptual front end: a gain of 7.00 [3.80, 10.21]. The complete method also improves control on $9\times9$ and in MiniWorld. Probes of remembered, out-of-view arrivals show stronger reward anticipation in both visual implementations. Concentration, tactile recovery and real tactile frames demonstrate complementary roles of remembered facts in imagined-policy training, motion-dependent decisions and sensory-content retention. EPM connects persistent perception to the predicted consequences and learning signals of a world model for Physical AI.



### Cross-Modal StateKV Injection for Communication in Multi-LLM Systems across Temporal Segments
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22304v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22304v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:02:09+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:02:09+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaoqian Zhang, Zhihao Zhang
- **Comment**: 9 pages, 4 figures, 5 tables. Equal contribution: Xiaoqian Zhang and Zhihao Zhang
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video understanding with multiple multimodal language models requires a communication interface that can transfer complementary visual observations. Text-based communication is interpretable, but introduces an intermediate generation stage and can omit information relevant to subsequent reasoning. We study Cross-Modal StateKV Injection, a provider--carrier framework that communicates through key--value (KV) states at visual-token positions. Provider models observe complementary video frames; their visual KV states are aligned and projected into the carrier representation space and integrated through gated residual updates. Injection preserves the carrier KV-cache sequence length, while retaining the additional computation required by the providers and the communication module. The reported experiments on MLVU and Video-MME compare carrier-only inference, text-based communication, and StateKV with two provider architectures. Results show modest, task-dependent accuracy gains and lower reported latency than the evaluated text-communication baseline, with some categories remaining unchanged or decreasing. Increasing provider-side frame coverage offers additional gains on selected tasks without extending the carrier cache. These findings motivate further study of visual KV communication under explicitly controlled observation and computation budgets.



### Inertial Video World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22314v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22314v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:09:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:09:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuan Gao, Fan Xu, Yu Guo, Yuxu Lu, Zhengru Fang, Hao Wu, Xiaomeng Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The physical world has inertia: it evolves from what already exists rather than being recreated at every instant. However, many existing video world models lack an explicit mechanism for inertial state evolution, leaving the continuity of motion and structure implicit in video prediction. During autoregressive rollouts, repeated reconstruction can accumulate errors and destabilize the predicted dynamics. To address this limitation, we introduce InertialWorld, an Inertial Video World Model that makes inertia a structural prior for autoregressive prediction: existing scene evidence is carried across time rather than reconstructed at every step. Central to this formulation is Inertial Memory, which turns memory from passive predictive context into an evolving video state with two complementary views: a dense Eulerian grid preserves scene-aligned evidence, while persistent Lagrangian carriers bind local content to learned canonical coordinates. At each step, the model transports the carrier content according to action-conditioned motion, predicts the complete next video state from the transported carriers and dense grid, and uses a learned gate to combine transported and newly predicted content into the memory for the next step. Extensive experiments across multiple domains, including robot manipulation, 3D game simulation, and open-world navigation, demonstrate that InertialWorld achieves competitive autoregressive prediction performance against strong video world model baselines. Codes link: https://github.com/YuanGao-YG/InertialWorld



### SIDUMN: Self-Supervised Image Denoising Under Symmetric Multiplicative Noise
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22317v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22317v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:16:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:16:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Nyan Htet Naing, Gyeongha Hwang
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables. Submitted to IEEE Signal Processing Letters
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Self-supervised denoising is challenging when the corruption is multiplicative and signal-dependent. In this letter, we propose SIDUMN, a symmetric noisier framework for self-supervised denoising under mean-one symmetric multiplicative noise. Given noisy observations and the noise law, the method samples an independent synthetic noise factor from the same law and constructs two complementary noisier images by applying the factor and its reflection about one. The denoising network is trained to predict one noisier image from the other, and at inference the reconstructions of $K$ such antithetic pairs are averaged. We show that the population target of the training loss is the clean conditional mean minus a multiplicative cross-term. We further prove that, for any trained network, antithetic averaging never increases the expected squared error of a single noisier-input reconstruction. Numerical results validate the theoretical findings and the proposed method.



### Breaking Visual Conventions: Spectrally Coupled Weight Perturbation for Creative Image Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22318v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22318v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:16:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:16:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Fu Feng, Ruoheng Zhou, Yucheng Xie, Xu Yang, Jing Wang, Xin Geng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-to-image (T2I) models often produce high-quality images that follow familiar visual conventions, even in response to open-ended prompts such as ``a creative [CLS]''. Existing methods encourage novelty by exploring semantic conditions or modifying features and guidance during denoising. Pretrained weights offer a complementary source of creative variation, but unstructured perturbations can have little visual effect or degrade image quality. We introduce Coupled Spectral Perturbation (CosPert), a training-free method that targets attention projections in layers identified by a one-time sensitivity analysis. For each projection, CosPert uses a shared random transformation and its transpose to couple updates to the singular-vector factors while perturbing the spectral gains. A common relative budget calibrates the magnitude of weight changes across projections, and a sigmoid schedule concentrates their influence in early denoising and gradually attenuates them during refinement. CosPert provides plug-and-play creativity enhancement at inference time. Once the perturbations are prepared, sampling requires no auxiliary networks or additional model evaluations. On both FLUX.1 and Stable Diffusion 3.5, CosPert enhances visual creativity, producing more varied object structures while preserving category identity. Across 50 object categories, CosPert achieves the highest mean CREward among the evaluated methods while maintaining strong prompt alignment.



### M$^3$-Test: Coverage Gaps in Joint-Perturbation Testing of Visual Question Answering
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22329v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22329v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:27:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:27:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Enhui Wan, Xiaowan Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A visual question answering (VQA) model can pass every image-only and question-only test yet fail when the same changes occur together. Standard robustness reports average over conditions and therefore cannot say which accepted requests remain vulnerable. We introduce M3-Test, a coverage audit that aligns each clean input with its single-modality variants and a complete 5x5 family, 4x4 strength joint library, so that any reduced test suite can be replayed on the same requests. Across 2.6 million responses from four vision-language models, joint failures affect 26-29% of the Qwen questions that pass all 40 single-modality tests, although fewer than 0.6% of their joint records fail. Passing the 25 maximum-strength corners does not close this gap: 11-14% of the accepted questions still fail elsewhere in the library. Re-executing the archived failures reproduces about 90% of them, against a 0.3-0.6% flip rate among matched passing controls, and re-checking the most fragile failure records supports 80-89% of them. On the evidence examined, neither execution noise nor grading error can fully account for the gap. The same aligned grids make previous evaluations reusable. On held-out images, a risk order learned from other models discovers about 80% of affected Qwen questions within 25 additional queries, compared with 65-68% for maximum-first and 53-57% for pairwise covering arrays. M3-Test thus turns a passing robustness suite into a request-level account of what remains untested, supporting acceptance, regression review and test allocation.



### DAP-MR: Degradation-Aware Prompt-Magnitude Routing for Real-World Ultrasound Super-Resolution
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22331v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22331v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:28:01+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:28:01+00:00
- **Authors**: Ngoc-Hoang-Lam Le, Thi-An Le, Dinh-Hung Le, Manh-Hung Nguyen, Kai-Lung Hua, Ching-Chun Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: When super-resolution models designed for natural images are used on ultrasound scans, relying on semantic text prompts can lead to unreliable results. The tags these models generate often do not match the actual restoration needs, and specialized medical terms may be misunderstood by general-purpose text encoders. Instead, prompts focused on common image degradations are more directly useful for guiding restoration. However, using a single prompt to describe both the type and severity of image degradation can entangle these factors, making it hard for the model to know how strong the degradation is. To address this limitation, we introduce Degradation-Aware Prompt--Magnitude Routing (DAP-MR). Each degradation type is represented by a dedicated token initialized from the contextualized EOS embedding of a degradation-oriented prompt and subsequently adapted during ultrasound SR training. A separate magnitude router predicts a continuous, input-dependent magnitude that modulates the corresponding token. Thus, language provides a degradation-aware initialization for what should be restored, while magnitude routing supplies an explicit signal for how strongly it should influence restoration. DAP-MR can be added to both one-step and multi-step diffusion super-resolution frameworks without changing their core structures. Our experiments--on synthetic datasets with both in-domain and under anatomical-domain shift, as well as unpaired real-world ultrasound data--show that DAP-MR delivers better restoration quality and generalizes more consistently across different domains.



### PURE: A Diagnostic Benchmark for Multi-Granularity Multimodal Identity Matching
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22343v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22343v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:52:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:52:22+00:00
- **Authors**: Hengyu You, Xintong Wang, Jin Cao, Liang Ding
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Identity matching asks whether two records denote the same identity, yet the answer depends on the target granularity. Two records may match at the base level but not at the variant level: they share an underlying design while differing in attributes such as color or size. Final matching accuracy does not separately characterize the capabilities involved. To study this problem, we use e-commerce as a challenging real-world domain. We introduce PURE, a diagnostic benchmark for multi-granularity multimodal identity matching covering four capability dimensions: Perception, Understanding, Reasoning, and Evidence-grounded Judgment. The benchmark comprises 1,457 multiple-choice questions across 14 tasks, derived from real-world matching pairs and verified by two domain experts. Across ten recent MLLMs, overall scores range from 47.1% to 86.0%. Understanding receives the highest mean score and Judgment the lowest, with weaknesses in visual difference localization and variant-level rule judgment. Modality ablations show that models benefit from multimodal evidence but can incorrectly identify conflicts between matching records. In direct matching, most models recognize matching pairs more reliably than non-matching pairs at the base level, whereas the reverse holds at the variant level. PURE therefore reveals what aggregate matching accuracy hides: not only whether models judge a pair correctly, but where they struggle to interpret evidence and apply matching criteria.



### TetherMark: Instance-Level Watermark Authentication Against Cross-Instance Transplantation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22345v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CR**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22345v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 07:53:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 07:53:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Jin Wei, Rundong Li, Li Ma, Yikai Wang, Ruihao Yang, Jianxiong Wu, Xiaoyuan Duan, Yanbo Wang, Runyu Mao, Chen Fang, Chang Xu, Ping Chen, Jun Dai, Xiaoyan Sun
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robust watermarking is commonly verified by asking whether a hidden payload remains decodable after compression, geometric distortion, or generative editing. Localized and semantic extensions may additionally check whether an expected region or category is still present. These tests establish evidence validity or content consistency, but not whether the evidence still belongs to the particular visual instance registered by its owner. We expose cross-instance transplantation: an attacker extracts or estimates valid watermark evidence from one registered image and transfers it to a different image of the same category. The message can still decode, the expected content can remain present, and the result can look natural, even though the evidence has been reassigned to the wrong instance. To enable instance-level watermark authentication, we introduce TetherMark, a post-hoc watermark that combines two complementary checks. The message check verifies an edit-robust payload, while the relation check uses secret patch-pair selection, a registered response, natural correspondence transport, and enrollment normalization to test consistency with the registered instance. The verifier accepts only when both checks pass. Across 240 test subjects, TetherMark attains 92.08% legitimate true acceptance with 9.17% and 16.25% attack success under WmForger and WMCopier, respectively, yielding the highest instance-binding balanced accuracy under both attacks.



### Scaling Laws for Vision-Language Fusion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22351v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22351v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 08:01:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 08:01:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Seng Pei Liew, Teppei Suzuki, Seiya Tokui
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) have emerged as central foundation models combining multimodal perception with text comprehension. While VLMs are traditionally built via late fusion by continued pre-training an off-the-shelf language model on multimodal (image-text) data, mixing modalities from the start of pre-training (early fusion) has recently gained traction. However, the fundamental trade-offs between these paradigms remain poorly understood, resulting in expensive and heuristic-driven training recipes. In this paper, we systematically analyze the learning dynamics of these multimodal pre-training processes, showing that validation losses during vision-language fusion can be modeled by separating the base learning trajectory, where different data types contribute at distinct rates, from a distribution shift driven by the competition between modalities. We derive a scaling law that unifiedly describes these dynamics, enabling accurate loss predictions across mixture ratios, training steps, and model sizes. Crucially, our law characterizes the trade-off between early and late fusion: under a fixed data budget, early fusion preserves text performance while late fusion favors vision performance, yielding a trade-off that increasingly tilts toward early fusion as model size scales. Extensive experiments confirm that our scaling law reliably captures multimodal learning dynamics and governs fusion decisions.



### SandBAR: A Band-Asymmetric Architecture for Underwater Image Restoration with Removable Frequency Scaffolding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22355v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22355v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 08:08:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 08:08:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Yanzi Liu, Haoran Hou, Jiachi Tong, Shen Chen, Shuang Chen
- **Comment**: Accepted to WACV 2027. Camera-ready version. 9 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Light scattering, absorption and turbidity degrade underwater imagery through complementary frequency tendencies: large-scale, channel-dependent appearance distortions and local high-frequency detail loss. This band asymmetry motivates different forms of restoration for global colour and local structure. Our primary contribution is SandBAR, a novel unified band-asymmetric architecture for underwater image restoration. Rather than treating frequency processing as an isolated module, SandBAR organises the restoration pipeline around band asymmetry at the block, encoder and objective levels. Within this architecture, we introduce a novel band-asymmetric sandwich block with a reserved post-attention repair slot and the Split-band Parallel Asymmetric Repair Kernel (SPARK), a novel module that applies channel-aware large-support correction to the low band, which predominantly carries large-scale appearance distortions, and lightweight local refinement to the detail-bearing high band, thereby restoring global colour while preserving fine structure. To deliver the global cast to the depth at which the network represents global structure, a learnable low-frequency guidance path injects a low-pass view of the input into the deep encoder, and a three-scale spatial-frequency objective supervises the same axis throughout the decoder. Building on the reserved post-attention slot, we further introduce Post-attention Scaffold Training (PaST), a novel removable nonlinear scaffolding scheme that trains the permanent SandBAR graph under band-specific capacity and runs it at inference without that capacity. SandBAR reaches 29.89 / 28.92 / 22.77 dB on EUVP / LSUI / UIEB and establishes a new three-domain mean state of the art on the benchmark family, exceeding the strongest published result by approximately 1.0 dB. PaST adds no parameters or FLOPs at inference and preserves matched latency.



### Can Vision-Language Models Truly Understand Images? A Benchmark Beyond Commonsense Bias
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22360v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22360v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 08:13:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 08:13:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Ling Lin, Heng Su, Xiangzeng Liu, Yang Bai, Congcong Zhu, Jingrun Chen
- **Comment**: 10 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved impressive performance on a wide range of vision-language tasks. However, it remains unclear whether their predictions are grounded in the visual content itself or are largely driven by statistical co-occurrence patterns and commonsense correlations learned from large-scale image-text corpora. Existing vision-language benchmarks predominantly consist of samples that conform to human commonsense, allowing models to achieve strong performance by exploiting dataset priors rather than genuinely understanding image content. To address this limitation, we present ContraSense, a systematically constructed benchmark for evaluating faithful vision-language understanding. ContraSense consists of carefully curated counter-intuitive image-text pairs that deliberately violate common expectations, substantially reducing the effectiveness of statistical biases and requiring models to rely on visual evidence for reasoning. We conduct comprehensive evaluations on a diverse set of representative open- and closed-source MLLMs. The results reveal a substantial performance gap between current MLLMs and humans: even the strongest closed-source model achieves only about 60\% accuracy, compared with 96.04\% for human participants. These findings demonstrate that current MLLMs still struggle with faithful visual understanding and highlight the need for more robust multimodal reasoning grounded in image content. The ContraSense benchmark is publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/LingLin00/ContraSense.



### Seeing What Still Holds: How Waiting Control Changes Cooperative Recovery
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22368v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22368v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 08:21:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 08:21:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Kangjie Huang, Yucheng Xin, Xinchun Wang, Dong Liu, Yan Fan, Shuming Liu
- **Comment**: 8 pages, 3figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Changing the holding reference during a fixed-duration wait can reverse the ranking of visual recovery methods. We introduce AuditEmbodied, a paired execution and trajectory evaluation framework that independently varies holding references and waiting duration while keeping recovery executors frozen. A 960-execution factorial on 20 initialization seeds compares CAD-feedback and landmark-based recovery from identical post-wait physical states within each holding and waiting condition. Protected completion requires successful recovery under the declared execution constraints. At a 1 s wait with registered wrist references, their protected completion counts change from 6/40 versus 19/40 under registered holding to 34/40 versus 22/40 under position-only holding. Trajectory analysis localizes the main separation to grasp formation: small observation-time target residuals precede large tracking errors. Joint diagnostics and controlled interventions characterize persistent tracking constraints. On fresh seeds, gradual position reanchoring improves CAD recovery's long-wait completion by 42.5 percentage points and reduces mean complete recovery time by 6.06 s relative to registered holding. Additional evaluations with pretrained policies, handover, and a mass-matched geometry characterize holding-reference sensitivity across executors and physical regimes. These findings establish holding reference as an explicit evaluation condition for comparing visual recovery performance.



### Leveraging Segmentation Representations for Efficient Classification: A Self-Configuring Framework Based on nnU-Net
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22380v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22380v1)
