In this paper, we introduce BRepA, a unified self-supervised pretraining framework that explicitly reconstructs both the geometry and topology of B-Rep objects via graph-based modeling. By learning from large-scale unlabeled CAD data only once, BRepA produces representations that generalize across tasks and significantly enhance both discriminative and generative learning. On segmentation tasks, BRepA demonstrates remarkable data efficiency. For instance, with only 1% labeled samples, BRepA pretraining improves mIoU by 32.52% compared to models trained from scratch. On generative tasks, aligning diffusion model representations with pretrained BRepA features substantially accelerates convergence, achieving nearly 2 times faster training while improving generation quality under the same computational budget, despite the diffusion model being roughly 20 times larger than BRepA. Overall, our results show that geometry- and topology-aware B-Rep pretraining provides a powerful foundation for both understanding and generating CAD structures, serving as a versatile representation backbone for diverse downstream applications. We will release the code to facilitate future research.



### SWIR-cGAN: Synthesizing SWIR Bands Using VNIR Imagery from CBERS and AMAZONIA-1
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22922v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/LGRS.2026.3753509
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22922v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:22:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:22:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Alisson Cleiton de Oliveira, Rennan de Freitas Bezerra Marujo, Thales Sehn Körting
- **Comment**: Available on: https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/11793642. Early Access
- **Journal**: IEEE Geoscience and Remote Sensing Letters, 2026
- **Summary**: Spectral band synthesis is an important research topic in remote sensing. The Brazilian satellites CBERS-4, CBERS-4A, and AMAZONIA-1 provide high temporal resolution (1-3 days) visible and near-infrared (VNIR) imagery through the Wide-Field Imager (WFI), but lack short-wave infrared (SWIR) bands. Recent deep learning methods have shown promising results for spectral synthesis, but challenges remain for cross-satellite generalization and spectrally distant bands. This paper presents a conditional Generative Adversarial Network (cGAN) with an attention-enhanced U-net generator and a multi-loss objective to synthesize Sentinel-2/MSI SWIR bands 11 (SWIR-1) and 12 (SWIR-2) from WFI VNIR inputs. Experiments used 4,125 patches for training and cross-validation and 1,026 unseen patches for testing, with hyperparameters optimized using a subset of the training data. On SWIR-1, the model achieved SSIM of 0.900, PSNR of 31.44 dB, and MAE of 0.019 in TOA reflectance. On SWIR-2, it achieved SSIM of 0.917, PSNR of 33.35 dB, and MAE of 0.015. Benchmarking was performed on the test dataset, with each model optimized separately prior to evaluation. SWIR-cGAN outperformed the baselines, particularly for SWIR-2. The ablation study identified the multi-loss as the main performance contributor, while the attention and residual modules provided additional improvements. Our results demonstrate the feasibility of synthesizing SWIR bands from WFI imagery. The SWIR-cGAN is available at https://github.com/marujore/swir-cgan.



### Code World Model 2.0: Towards Infinite Controllable Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22925v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22925v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:23:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 05:27:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Ruoyu Wang, Hao Zhong, Yanming Yang, Jiahao Tian, Lin Liu, Biao Gong, Guoshan Lu, Yiding Tian, Chunhua Shen, Zhenzhong Lan, Chi Zhang
- **Comment**: Project Page: https://www.inclusion-ai.org/CodeWorldModel/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual proxies decouple explicit world-state simulation from photorealistic video synthesis, offering a controllable interface for both offline clip creation and real-time interactive streaming. Yet a practical renderer must address two challenges. Upstream proxies vary from coarse layouts to fine articulated geometry depending on the interaction, and streaming inference must operate in a few causal steps without accumulating drift across self-generated history. We present Invoker-Render, pairing a bidirectional creation model with a causal four-step streaming model under a shared conditioning interface over structural proxies, an appearance reference, and language. Trained on an aligned approximately 200-hour multi-granularity corpus, Invoker-Render honors fine geometric constraints when supplied while synthesizing plausible local motion under coarse proxies, enabling runtime selection of input detail. To bridge bidirectional creation and real-time streaming without compounding step compression and rollout drift, we introduce Progressive Forcing (ProForcing). ProForcing first trains a causal predictor and distills it into a four-step renderer using histories derived from training videos. Training then shifts to continuous self-generated rollouts. A pipelined serving stack supports efficient streaming inference. Experiments show versatile control across proxy families, granularities, and camera paths, consistent gains over recent streaming baselines, and stable 30-minute racing rollouts that preserve scene and subject appearance.



### A high-dimensional spatial-physical coupling operator for generalizable super-resolution of three-dimensional turbulent flows
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22951v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **physics.flu-dyn**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22951v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:41:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:41:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Zengyuan Zuo, Junjun Jiang, Fei Mu, Xubo Wang, Xianming Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Accurate representation of turbulent flows requires simultaneously capturing multi-scale spatial structures and the complex, nonlinear coupling between distinct physical quantities (\textit{e.g.}, velocity components and density). In practice, however, turbulent data are often constrained by low spatial resolution due to hardware limitations and computational costs. While current data-driven super-resolution methods have shown promise by learning mappings via convolutional neural networks (CNNs), they typically treat physical variables as independent channels. This simplified approach neglects intrinsic cross-variable correlations, causing models to struggle with high-fidelity reconstruction, particularly under extremely sparse conditions or in out-of-distribution regimes. To bridge this gap, we propose an architecture-agnostic spatial-physical coupling operator that extends the standard convolution into a joint spatial-variable dimension. Unlike conventional linear channel mixing, our framework constructs a unified spatial-physical representation within CNNs to explicitly capture local inter-variable interactions. By lifting features into a higher-dimensional manifold, this operator significantly enhances the model's representation capacity. Experiments demonstrate that this approach enables strong reconstruction performance and zero-shot generalization, offering a physics-aware inductive bias for super-resolution of three-dimensional turbulence. More broadly, this work suggests that high-dimensional operators offer a promising avenue for modeling intricate multi-scale turbulent flows.



### Fiberpose: state-of-the-art fiber morphometry and instance segmentation with a distance-transform neural network
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22953v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, q-bio.QM
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22953v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:41:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:41:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Viktor A. Balashov, Daniil S. Antonenko
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Analysis of fibrous structures in SEM images is a routine task in biomedical engineering and materials science. However, most available methods for fiber quantification still rely on classical image binarization followed by simple post-processing. These approaches often produce unreliable results in images with weak boundaries, heterogeneous contrast, and densely packed fibers. Here, we introduce Fiberpose, a framework that reformulates fiber analysis as direct regression of a smooth distance-transform (DT) field, together with a novel set of geometry-based algorithms specifically adapted to this representation. The predicted field directly encodes fiber geometry, while the accompanying algorithms enable instance reconstruction and measurement of diameter and orientation within a unified workflow. The framework is implemented as a standalone Python desktop application and a web tool https://fiberpose.org. On an independent dataset of fibrous SEM images, Fiberpose achieved state-of-the-art instance-segmentation accuracy in comparison with SAM2 and Cellpose-SAM, and more accurate diameter measurements than DiameterJ, Fibar and GIFT. The method remains effective in dense and weakly contrasted regions and shows qualitative generalization across microscopy modalities not represented in the training data. Fiberpose therefore provides an interpretable geometric representation and an automated pipeline for reproducible quantitative characterization of fibrous materials.



### Is model self-assessment enough for diverse LVLM ensembles?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22956v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22956v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:43:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:43:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Michael Smith, Frank P. Ferrie
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Ensembles of diverse Large Vision Language Models have demonstrated performance beyond that of single models across multiple datasets and tasks. However, the necessary aggregation process usually relies on training, parameter fitting, or complex task-specific pipelines. Furthermore, evaluation is limited, making a fair comparison difficult. We create a unified pipeline where we perform candidate response selection: we build an ensemble by selecting the best answer from a set provided by different models. We comprehensively evaluate across 34 different means of confidence estimation, 24 ensemble configurations, 20 datasets, and two prompt specifications. We show that simple training and parameter-free approaches can be sufficient, even for ensembles of different architectures. These simple self-assessment approaches rely solely on each constituent model's confidence, without explicit calibration, yet are able to perform better than said constituent models. They are also competitive with more involved alternatives on standard metrics such as accuracy, including on open-ended problems. However, we also discover that dataset, ensemble composition, and prompt wording are key confounding variables insufficiently explored by prior work that significantly affect ensemble performance, such that the ensemble can perform worse than its best constituent model. These must be optimized for to get the most out of ensembles, at the cost of limiting their generalizability. We recommend that practitioners consider training-free ensembles only if deployment conditions can be precisely characterized, with Best-of-N configurations of strong models being a good starting point.



### Hierarchical Diffusion Transformer for End-to-End Image Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22962v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22962v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:47:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:47:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Wu, Yifei Wang, Weihang Guo, Chen Wei, Lydia E Kavraki, Niloofar Mireshghallah, Steven Wu
- **Comment**: Under Review
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pixel-space diffusion learns visual representations and image generation jointly in a single end-to-end training stage, without a separately pretrained visual encoder. However, plain pixel-space Transformers still trail leading two-stage latent diffusion models in generation quality and training efficiency. We hypothesize that part of this gap arises from retaining one token per patch throughout the backbone, which does not explicitly encourage a visual hierarchy that summarizes related content at progressively coarser levels. To address this, we introduce Hierarchical Diffusion Transformers (HiT), which progressively group related tokens and represent each group with a single token. The main Transformer bottleneck processes these compact group tokens, and ungrouping restores features to their original image positions. A compression budget regulates the average compression ratio across the batch, allowing group memberships and counts to vary across images and diffusion timesteps during both training and generation. The model exhibits emergent semantic structure, learning to group coherent objects and object parts without segmentation labels. In text-to-image generation, HiT further learns to group caption words with image patches depicting the same concepts, without region annotations. HiT achieves 1.48 FID on ImageNet $256\times256$ and reaches comparable generation quality to JiT using approximately 40\% of its cumulative training FLOPs. For text-to-image generation, HiT exceeds UniLiP's average score across five benchmarks with roughly $6\times$ fewer denoiser parameters and $3\times$ fewer forward FLOPs. These results suggest that learning a visual hierarchy within the generator breaks down the barrier to stronger image generation without external visual pretraining.



### Training-Free Building Change Detection with Example-Induced Discriminants
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22964v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22964v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:51:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:51:05+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhi Lu, Bin Hong, Zhenming Peng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Building change detection (BCD) relies on dense annotations that are costly to collect across regions. A few annotated examples offer a more economical way to specify the target, but change maps provide incomplete building supervision: they label only changed buildings and leave the building-containing date unspecified. Our key observation is that these annotations nevertheless reveal features that recognize buildings in other images, including unchanged ones. We present Linear INduced Discriminants (LIND), a training-free framework that turns regional feature contrasts into a reusable linear building classifier. Using a single frozen vision transformer, it unifies building recognition and temporal comparison in the same feature space. This approach requires neither text prompts nor an auxiliary segmentation model. Only one parameter is tuned on a single validation dataset and then kept fixed across datasets and shot settings. Extensive experiments demonstrate strong detection accuracy, cross-dataset transfer, and efficient inference, with extensions to other semantic classes and broader land-cover changes. Change annotations thus provide a compact visual specification for detection in new scenes. Code: https://github.com/lzcn/lind



### CodeReel: Accelerating Code-to-Video Agents with Exact Frame Reuse and Blocking-Aware Scheduling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22967v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.MM**, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.DC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22967v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:52:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:52:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Junyi Shen, Yao Lu
- **Comment**: 19 pages, 8 figures, 14 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Coding agents make videos by writing and refining programs in a build--inspect--edit loop. A finished film costs a median of $2.85 in model calls and 11 minutes, over a third of that time in builds that block the agent. We find that much of this time is avoidable, for two reasons. First, 37.5% of the frames of a session's later films were drawn before, many shifted in time or inside an edited scene. Second, half of the builds stop at a check, where the agent needs only the verdict, but which builds stop is unknown until the checks finish. We present CodeReel, a training-free execution layer behind agents' existing commands. Exact frame reuse keys each frame by its drawing commands, regardless of its position in time, and reuses each frame whose key recurs. Blocking-aware scheduling runs the checks first and starts the render alongside them, at idle priority, unless the session's recent builds make a pass unlikely. Scheduling spends CPU to answer sooner and reuse returns it; exact keys let later versions use frames rendered for a failed one as the agent thinks. We introduce EditLoop, a benchmark of replayable agent sessions whose films are checked against renders from scratch. On it, CodeReel cuts the agent's wait by 36.3% against Rebuild, a warm pipeline with parallel rendering, and by 57.9% against Sequential, today's step-by-step pipeline. On time-shifting edits its reuse renders 43.9%, close to the 43.0% whose pixels are new, while Manim-style caching renders 99.8%. With real agents, the median wait per build is 0.62x Rebuild's in Claude Code, on topics never used in development, and 0.67x in Codex CLI. The gain holds for five models and two frameworks.



### MI-Hyper: Generating Multiple Instance Models for Weight Space Transfer Learning in Histopathology via Mixed Tasks Pretrained HyperNetwork
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22968v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22968v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:52:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:52:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Xitong Ling, Yousef Kotp, Weiming Chen, Yining Li, Jiawen Li, Minxi Ouyang, Xinyi Guo, Mingxi Fu, Yizhi Wang, Tian Guan, Lianghui Zhu, Yonghong He
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Zero-shot whole-slide image (WSI) prediction aims to generalize to new pathology tasks without parameter updates at deployment. MI-Zero makes these predictions by pooling patch-text similarities. Conditional weight generation, combined with supervision from diverse pathology tasks, offers an alternative: learning to construct predictive rules from task descriptions. We introduce MI-Hyper, a hypernetwork pretrained across mixed tasks to generate the aggregation and classification weights of multiple instance learning (MIL) models. We first train the generator and a shared MIL basis jointly using source labels. We then freeze the basis and refine the generator. The generated task weights are reused across cases. Across 308 tasks from 52 pathology datasets, MI-Hyper improves mean AUC, balanced accuracy and macro-F1 over MI-Zero by 1.85, 1.17 and 0.57 percentage points, respectively. On some molecular prediction tasks, both AUC and balanced accuracy improve by more than 20 points. These results support task-conditioned weight generation as a promising approach to weight-space transfer for long-sequence modeling.



### Rescaling OLS-Merged Adapters To Improve Personalized Image Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22969v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22969v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 15:52:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 15:52:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Leander Girrbach, Niklas Schweiger, Zeynep Akata
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Merging multiple adapters into a single image generation model reduces deployment costs while preserving diverse task capabilities or personalization modes. State-of-the-art methods such as RegMean, Mix-of-Show, and Subspace Routing fit the outputs of the merged model to those of the individual adapters using ordinary least squares (OLS). We show that this reconstruction objective systematically shrinks task-specific signals whenever the activation distributions of different tasks overlap, producing a merged adapter that is too weak for downstream use. We derive a decomposition that quantifies this shrinkage and propose rescaling the merged update to restore signal strength. On multi-subject personalization with up to 30 merged adapters, rescaling substantially improves subject fidelity over unscaled baselines. Our approach also improves results on style transfer, concept erasure, image classification, and LLM-based text classification.



### SpikWM: Efficient End-to-End Spiking World Modeling for Visual Planning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22981v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22981v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:00:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:00:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Shuhan Ye, Hongbin Yu, Chong Wang, Hui Lu, Jiaming He, Yi Yu, Jiangbo Qian, Xudong Jiang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Goal-conditioned planning with image observations and goals requires predictions that distinguish visual states and action consequences. Spiking neural networks offer sparse computation, but single-step spikes can obscure motion cues and merge actions with different physical outcomes. We propose the Spiking Latent World Model (SpikWM), an end-to-end joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) with one simulation step per image and one neural update per latent position. Spiking Regional Encoding retains spatial cues through regional pooling. Bi-Spike Action Encoding combines positive and negative binary responses, while Causal Membrane Prediction carries subthreshold evidence across the latent history with learned group-wise time constants. The encoder and predictor are trained jointly on reward-free image--action trajectories using next-embedding prediction and SIGReg. With a common planner, SpikWM achieves mean success rates of $88.67\%$, $97.33\%$, $99.33\%$, and $86.67\%$ on Push-T, Two-Room, Reacher, and OGBench-Cube, respectively, outperforming two spiking baselines. Push-T ablations support each module, and controlled replays examine responses to appearance and physical state changes. Under a matched planning budget, SpikWM reduces estimated arithmetic energy by $75.6\%$ relative to LeWM. Code will be released at https://github.com/ShawnYE618/SpikWM.



### CAST: Calibrating Correspondences for Training-Free Biomedical In-Context Segmentation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22995v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22995v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:10:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:10:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Mingze Jiang, Jiashu Han, Haonan Chen, Zhiyi Li, Yilun Du, Kunzan Liu
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Biomedical image segmentation must keep up with new anatomical structures, subjects, imaging modalities, and acquisition protocols, yet dense expert annotations are scarce and models trained on one cohort often struggle to transfer. In-context segmentation (ICS) offers a way out: a new structure is specified by a few annotated exemplars at inference time. However, reliable correspondence remains challenging in biomedical images, where adjacent structures often share similar appearances, and variations across subjects and imaging conditions further obscure their distinctions. We introduce CAST, a training-free framework built on a frozen DINOv3 backbone to calibrate fine-grained correspondences. Its stability test improves feature reliability at both global and patch-specific levels, while its coherence test assesses whether candidate correspondences are supported by consistent local structural context. Together, these tests enable more reliable correspondence across diverse anatomical structures and imaging conditions. Across eight biomedical benchmarks spanning 2D and 3D segmentation, cross-subject, and cross-modality transfer, CAST outperforms the strongest overall baseline by 12.34 percentage points in Dice and ranks first across all six volumetric settings. The versatility of CAST further opens up opportunities for challenging biomedical applications, including 2D-to-3D segmentation and atlas label transfer.



### The Rhythm of Generated Video: Latent Chunking Leaves Periodic Traces in Compressed Bitstreams
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22996v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.22996v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:11:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:11:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Chen Ye Gan, Bryan Zhen Zhang, Gene Wen, Yuxing han
- **Comment**: Manuscript, 9 pages, Computer vision, AI-generated video detection, compressed-domain analysis, video coding,
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Most AI-generated video detectors analyze decoded pixels and overlook how videos are produced. We show that causal video autoencoders leave an architecture-specific rhythm in compressed bitstreams: their temporal stride concentrates unpredictable content at latent-chunk boundaries, so rate-distortion optimized encoders allocate bits periodically. Without decoding pixels, we observe four-frame pulses in CogVideoX and Wan2.2 and eight-frame pulses in LTX-Video, which are absent from content-matched camera footage. Passing camera footage through a generator's autoencoder alone induces the pulse, supporting its proposed origin. A training-free peak-ratio statistic separates generated from camera video (AUC 0.84-0.94) and persists across tested quantization settings and H.264/H.265 encoding. Frame-size traces also identify four commercial generators, with 89.2%, 84.1%, and 83.0% accuracy under H.264, H.265, and AV1, beating the pixel-based UnivFD baseline (72.7-73.6%). Our results directly link generative architecture to measurable coding demand.



### Tracking Ethological Behaviors of Broiler Chickens Through Computer Vision (YoloV11) Using an Android Mobile Device
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23007v1
- **DOI**: 10.22456/2175-2745.151469
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23007v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:19:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:19:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Ryan Davi Oliveira de Meneses, Glauber da Rocha Balthazar
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: This study developed and evaluated a computer vision model for automated ethological behavior classification of broiler chickens in climate-controlled poultry houses. Using publicly available videos preprocessed and manually annotated into three classes (sitting, standing, and feeding), the YOLOv11 model was trained and deployed on an Android application via TFLite conversion. Exploratory analysis ensured dataset consistency and visual quality, while evaluation showed class-dependent performance: higher robustness for static postures (sitting, AP=0.531; precision=100%) and lower accuracy for dynamic behaviors (feeding, AP=0.292; recall=0.20). Tests with unseen images demonstrated significant improvements, achieving accuracies of 98.04% for sitting, 83.33% for standing, and 67.65% for feeding. The model showed effective generalization in real farm environments, supporting its potential for continuous behavioral monitoring, early welfare assessments, and decision-making in precision poultry farming. These findings establish a technical foundation for future mobile-based applications at commercial scale, reinforcing the role of computer vision in automated animal welfare evaluation.



### When a Larger Guidance Scale Repairs Text-to-Video Failures and When It Does Not
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23025v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23025v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:30:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:30:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Taewon Kang, Matthias Zwicker
- **Comment**: 29 pages, 15 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: When a text-to-video diffusion model ignores part of a prompt, the usual first fix is to raise the classifier-free guidance scale. This paper is a measurement study of when that fix works. On Mochi, the same increase repairs failures on rare compositions ("a snowy beach") beyond what generating again does, does not repair negations ("a highway with no vehicles"), where it fixes fewer failures than generating again does, and changes nothing for events that must happen late in a clip ("a sandcastle that a wave washes flat"). Which of these happens can be known in advance, from the prompt and one inexpensive score. The score, which we call demand, measures how strongly the denoiser reacts to the part of the prompt that the failed video leaves out, and it is read from the first quarter of denoising, before any video exists. Our main finding is a condition: raising the scale is effective only when the prompt contains no negation and no event tied to a specific time, and when demand is sufficiently high, with recovery clearly higher above a fitted threshold than below it. Published corrections for the three failure types, used as fixed probes, behave as this account predicts. A short decision rule follows: before generating again, a user can tell whether the most common fix is worth trying, and what to use when it is not.



### Check Your Blind Spots: Decision-Guided Perception by Probing the Planner with Hypothetical Objects
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23030v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23030v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:33:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 07:46:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Hoang Pham Cong, Long D. Nguyen, Ho Viet Duc Luong, Nguyen Tran Minh Nhat, Binh P. Nguyen
- **Comment**: Source Code: https://github.com/kongwoang/DGSP
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Not every perception error changes the planner's action. Yet adaptive-resolution perception chooses where to spend high-resolution computation from image cues, which can neither reliably point to an object that low resolution missed nor tell which objects matter to the planner. We observe that the planner can say where an object would matter before anything is seen: inserting a hypothetical object at a location tests whether its action would change. Decision-Guided Speculative Perception (DGSP) treats the low-resolution scene and its action as a draft and probes the planner in this way to obtain a decision-sensitivity field, the locations where an unseen object would change the action. Only this field and the support, the detections on which the current action rests, are verified at high resolution, repeatedly until the action is stable. For two planner classes, we prove that DGSP returns exactly the action of whole-frame high-resolution perception if crop detections match whole-frame ones and the crops cover the field, conditions that real images meet approximately. On three driving datasets, it reproduces this action on about 98% of frames with about a quarter of the pixels. At the pixel cost of uniform downscaling, it repairs more of the draft's disagreements with this action, brakes falsely less often, and recovers many more of the decision-relevant objects that the draft misses.



### M3OPD-Reasoner: An Open Recipe for Post-Training Multimodal Reasoning with Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23034v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23034v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:36:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 16:10:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Nguyen Quang Trung, Hoang Phan, Shuo Sun, Longyin Zhang, AiTi Aw
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation (MOPD) enables a single student to absorb the skills of several domain teachers by learning from its own generated responses. We present Multimodal Model Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation Reasoner (M3OPD-Reasoner), a fully open recipe for post-training vision-language models (VLMs) over three reasoning domains: image STEM, image perception, and video. We curate two datasets, M3OPD-SFT-1.35M for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and M3OPD-RL-193K for reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable rewards. On these data, we compare post-training recipes that start from Qwen3.5-9B: training one model on the mixed data of all domains (MixSFT and MixRL), and training one teacher per domain with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), directly or after an SFT warm-up, and distilling the teachers into one student with MOPD. We evaluate on 21 image STEM, image perception, and video reasoning benchmarks after we decontaminate the training data against them. The comparison establishes MOPD of teachers trained with GSPO directly from Qwen3.5-9B as the best recipe: the student closely matches each teacher on its own domain and is on average above the mixed-training baselines on the same prompts. Since MOPD already merges the domain capabilities that MixRL would otherwise have to learn, its student is a stronger starting point for RL, and we ask whether the same data can carry the model further from there: we add MixRL on top of the MOPD student, on the same prompts and with the same settings, which, to our knowledge, no prior VLM recipe has studied. The same prompts still improve the MOPD student, with no collapse, and give our main model, M3OPD-Reasoner-9B. MOPD and MixRL can therefore work as consecutive stages that get more out of the same data. We release all data, the training code, and the checkpoints of every stage.



### Automated Annotation-Free Synthetic Data Generation for Underwater Litter Detection: A Comparison of Generative Approaches
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23037v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23037v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:36:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:36:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Amala Mary Vincent, Bart De Schutter
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Underwater object detection is often limited by scarce and imbalanced training data and by strong appearance variations caused by turbidity, lighting, and differences between sensors. Existing data augmentation strategies, such as geometric or color augmentation or fully synthetic image generation with Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), either lack semantic realism, fail to respect scene physics, or require additional annotations. In low-data domains like underwater environmental monitoring, the need for additional annotations is often the core bottleneck. Collecting new footage is hard, but labeling this dataset is even more time-consuming and costly, which limits how quickly a usable dataset can be constructed. We propose a unified fully automated zero-annotation pipeline that inserts class-conditioned trash objects into real underwater frames while preserving the original background context. Starting from sampled bounding boxes, each box is converted into an insertion mask and the object is pasted or generated within it, while the same box coordinates are automatically written as the corresponding annotation. Because labels are derived directly from the sampled boxes, the pipeline generates fully labeled training data for low-data settings without any manual annotation. Within this single pipeline we compare three strategies: copy-paste, Stable Diffusion inpainting, and DALL-E image-conditioned generation. We evaluate bounding-box faithfulness, visual realism, and the impact of the generated samples on downstream detector performance on the SeaClear underwater debris dataset. Results show that DALL-E improves detection performance in four of five domains, with relative gains of up to 96 percent over the real-only baseline.



### When Anchors Shift the Target: Advantage-Gated Adaptation for Diffusion Reinforcement Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23038v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23038v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:37:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:37:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Pu Cao, Min Zhou, Ying Shu, Tiezheng Ge, Qing Song, Lu Yang
- **Comment**: 12 pages, 4 figures, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Forward-process reinforcement learning adapts diffusion and flow-matching models by re-noising rewarded generations into regression targets. Because negative advantages induce concave updates with unbounded repulsive gradients, existing methods stabilize training using cached predictions from a moving policy copy, yet these anchors also alter what positive feedback teaches the model to fit. We formalize this conflict as anchor coupling: after separating frozen-reference regularizers, affine moving-anchor rules cannot eliminate negative curvature while preserving every positive regression target. We propose Advantage-Gated Adaptation (AGA) to decouple these roles. Positive samples retain standard target fitting, whose self-limiting update vanishes at the observed target independently of the moving anchor. Negative samples instead use the first-order tangent of the concave objective at the moving-policy prediction, preserving the local repulsion direction with a prediction-independent gradient. Within the quadratic-anchor family, these requirements uniquely determine the parameter-free gate $ω(a)=\max(-a,0)$. Because the moving copy provides local linearization rather than retention, AGA assigns long-horizon behavior preservation to a frozen pretrained reference regularized at its operating classifier-free guidance scale, interpreted through an implicit-reward lens. Across compositional generation, text rendering, and preference alignment, our experiments show that displacing positive targets degrades visual quality and heightens sensitivity to policy-copy schedules, whereas AGA consistently improves the reward--quality trade-off.



### Uncovering the Invisible in Routine Radiology: Cross-Modal Detection of MRI-Verified Soft-Tissue Pathologies from CBCT via Physics-Guided 3D Self-Supervised Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23042v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23042v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:40:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:40:33+00:00
- **Authors**: Evgeny Alves Limarenko
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Routine X-ray modalities like cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) are optimized for high-contrast mineralized tissues, leaving soft-tissue abnormalities buried beneath photon noise and below human visual perception. Thus, detecting non-calcified pathologies requires secondary magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). We demonstrate that sub-visual radiographic signatures of soft-tissue pathology are physically preserved within routine CBCT scans and can be extracted for opportunistic screening using a targeted, physics-guided deep learning pipeline, even under extreme paired-data scarcity. Using the temporomandibular joint (TMJ) as a model system (N = 212 paired CBCT-MRI joints), we address the severe overfitting of high-capacity 3D networks through a two-stage framework. First, we employ a frozen full-grid 3D masked autoencoder (BertsWin) pre-trained on 6,550 unlabeled CBCT volumes via the GradientConductor (GCond) optimizer and an anatomy-aware objective (PhysLoss) redirecting gradient flow into the [-300, 300] HU soft-tissue window. Second, automated localization of the condylar zenith drives a deterministic 3D cropping that eliminates 81% of background osseous variance. Evaluated via a patient-isolated 3x10-fold stratified cross-validation protocol, a statistically robust sub-visual signal for MRI-verified synovial effusion (ROC AUC = 0.793) emerges exclusively from representations pre-trained with the PhysLoss and GCond regime. Crucially, only this setup captures true 3D anatomical chirality (p < 0.001) and high intra-patient structural consistency (0.791) over inter-patient noise; conversely, identical architectures pre-trained with standard L2 loss or unmasked variance decomposition (MVC) collapse to random guessing. These findings establish a generalizable paradigm for repurposing routine, low-contrast radiographic scans into opportunistic screening tools for occult soft-tissue disease.



### Wall-to-wall maps of canopy height and aboveground carbon across the Atlantic Forest
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23048v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23048v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:45:31+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 12:25:26+00:00
- **Authors**: Matheus Pinheiro Ferreira, Thiago Carmuega Rabaçal, Giulio Brossi Santoro, Giovanna de Andrade Ferreira, Rodrigo Costa Pinto, Danilo R. A. de Almeida, Paulo Guilherme Molin, Catherine Torres de Almeida, Angélica F. Resende, Vinicius Castro Souza, Marcelo Antonio Pinho Ferreira, Cássio Toledo, Eben North Broadbent, Angelica Almeyda Zambrano, Laura Simões, Juliano van Melis, Ricardo Ribeiro Rodrigues, Pedro H. S. Brancalion
- **Comment**: 56 pages, 13 figures, 8 tables, 2 supplementary tables. Submitted to Scientific Data. Maps and code openly available via Zenodo and GitHub
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Global canopy height maps are increasingly used to estimate forest carbon, yet their regional accuracy is rarely tested. We present wall-to-wall maps of canopy height (10 m) and aboveground carbon density (30 m) for the Brazilian Atlantic Forest in 2024. Height was predicted by a deep learning network fusing Sentinel-2 reflectance, Sentinel-1 and ALOS PALSAR backscatter and ALOS elevation, trained on 7,179 and tested on 1,793 patches (58,800 km$^2$) from a statewide airborne LiDAR campaign over São Paulo state, Brazil. On held-out test patches, the mean absolute error is 2.26 m at 10 m and 1.90 m at 30 m, and the model was validated at two LiDAR sites outside São Paulo. Over the same pixels, the Meta and World Resources Institute global product has more than twice this error. A power law fitted to 124 inventory plots converted canopy height to above ground carbon density. The remaining forest holds 1,944 TgC over 28.3 Mha. Global products therefore need local validation before use in carbon accounting.



### Refin3R: Geometry-Guided Refinement for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23051v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23051v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:46:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:46:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Jongmin Lee, Jaeyeong Kim, Seungryong Kim
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models predict dense point maps from multiple images in a single pass. Without explicit geometric constraints, however, their predictions are often inconsistent across views and lack fine-grained precision. Recent methods incorporate geometric priors such as camera pose or depth, but use them merely as auxiliary inputs, process each view independently, or refine the geometry only where the priors are available. We present Refin3R, which refines feed-forward point maps with Structure-from-Motion (SfM) points, propagating SfM geometry over the image grid. In its dual-branch architecture, a 2D branch extends SfM geometry beyond its coverage using geometry-aware backbone features, while a 3D branch reasons over the SfM points of all views, capturing multi-view structure that the image grid alone cannot represent. The two branches exchange information bidirectionally to jointly refine each view, yielding dense and accurate point maps. Across ScanNet++, ETH3D, Tanks\&Temples, and out-of-domain human and surgical scenes, Refin3R consistently outperforms feed-forward and geometry-guided methods in point map accuracy while running faster than the strongest geometry-guided baseline.



### ReTouchGS: Ultralight Render-free Aesthetic Color Enhancement and Transfer for 3D Gaussian Splatting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23054v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23054v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:47:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:47:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Chuanzhi Xu, Boyu Wei, Chengkun Yue, Xuanhua Yin, Yiyang Li, Haodong Chen, Qiang Qu, Weidong Cai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables photorealistic scene reconstruction. Existing 3DGS color transfer and interactive editing methods are guided by reference images and user-specified color adjustments. However, in many applications, users simply want aesthetically guided color enhancement that automatically makes the scene more visually appealing while maintaining consistent colors and tones across viewpoints. We present ReTouchGS, to our knowledge the first framework for reference-free aesthetic color enhancement of pretrained 3DGS models. It predicts a scene-level color lookup table (LUT) from the model and its associated camera parameters. ReTouchGS also supports reference-guided color transfer by fitting a scene-level LUT using one edited view of the same scene and its corresponding original render. A shared blend-aware color refinement network with only 44.7K parameters refines an analytic color update to realize either color mapping without rendering. It preserves geometry and delivers standard 3DGS assets in seconds. We also design the ReTouch3D benchmark to support the evaluation of aesthetic color enhancement and reference-guided color transfer across viewpoints. Experiments demonstrate high color accuracy and strong generalization to unseen scenes in both aesthetic color enhancement and reference-guided color transfer.



### FrameRouter: Frame Budget Routing for Long-Video Understanding on Video-MME-v2 and Beyond
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23055v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23055v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:49:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 16:49:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Bingjun Luo, Jialin Guo, Siqi Li
- **Comment**: Accepted to NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long-video question answering benchmarks like Video-MME-v2 typically evaluate systems under a uniform frame budget for every question, despite stark variation in the evidence each query actually demands. We analyze this mismatch on Video-MME-v2 and find that different question subsets prefer different frozen frame-budget policies, while uniformly increasing the frame count does not reliably improve all metrics. These findings motivate fixed-mean frame-budget routing, a controlled inference setting in which each question may receive a different frame budget while the average budget matches a uniform reference. We introduce FrameRouter, a training-free test-time framework that combines an evidence-demand router, a budget-constrained allocation rule, and a frozen bank of frame-sampling policies. By controlling how much visual evidence each question receives while leaving the underlying samplers unchanged, FrameRouter is orthogonal to query-aware frame selection. Across Video-MME-v2 and several long-video understanding benchmarks, FrameRouter improves long-video question answering by using the same average visual budget more selectively across questions. Code is available at https://github.com/bingjunluo/FrameRouter.



### RFW-S: Everyday Deformable Objects with Independent Simulation Verification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23056v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23056v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 16:49:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-18 11:00:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiarui Guo, Saining Zhang, Jiaying Wan, Jiawei Weng, Junhao Chen, Rui Zhang, Yuanbo Lyu, Zhiheng Lee, Peishuo Li, Jingrun Du, Weiqing Xiao, Weiyi Lin, Zenan Meng, Hu Wei, Lin Qu, Zongzheng Zhang, Hao Zhao, Chaojian Li
- **Comment**: 34 pages, 19 figures. Project Page: https://roboflywheel-com.github.io/RoboFlywheel-Soft/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Large-scale robotic simulation requires diverse deformable objects with executable physical properties and realistic interaction behaviors. However, existing 3D asset collections primarily emphasize visual diversity, while scalable physical construction and verification remain challenging. We introduce RFW-S, a large-scale dataset of 83,365 simulation-verified assets spanning 485 everyday categories across five structural families: soft solids, cloth sheets, shells, garments, and bags. We develop an automated pipeline integrating reference-guided 3D generation, structure-aware physical modeling, and LLM-assisted parameter estimation and calibration. To ensure physical reliability, we introduce an independent simulation verification framework that evaluates object behaviors under interactions distinct from those used for parameter calibration. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of independent verification and characterize deformable responses under different physical configurations. We further evaluate robotic grasping in terms of both task success and object integrity, and demonstrate the physical realism of our simulated objects through sim-to-real replay of fragile-object manipulation. RFW-S provides a scalable foundation for physically grounded simulation and embodied robotic learning.



### UnrealEvent: Synthetic Dataset Generation and Annotation for Event-Based Vision
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23078v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23078v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:04:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:04:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Yentel Schoovaerts, Zijie Ning, Patrick Vandewalle
- **Comment**: Accepted at the Workshop on Neuromorphic Vision (NeVi), ECCV 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Event-based cameras provide high temporal resolution, low power consumption, and a high dynamic range, but progress in event-based object detection remains constrained by the scarcity of annotated training data. To solve this issue, this paper introduces the tool UnrealEvent, a synthetic dataset-generation pipeline implemented in Unreal Engine 5.5. The pipeline combines a V2E-inspired event-frame generator with EasySynth semantic-rendering passes, allowing rendered level sequences to be converted into paired event representations and YOLO-format bounding-box annotations without manual labeling. Events are generated from per-pixel log-luminance differences between consecutive frames, with contrast thresholds sampled independently from a normal distribution to emulate the fabrication-induced threshold mismatch of real sensors. Using this pipeline, we provide a synthetic dataset and evaluate YOLOv8 detectors under synthetic, zero-shot real, and real fine-tuning training on eTraM. The results show strong in-domain synthetic performance but a substantial synthetic-to-real gap, especially for pedestrians. Fine-tuning on a small real subset improves real-domain performance, while the remaining gap highlights the importance of asset diversity and sensor calibration.



### Transfer Once, Ask Locally: Compact Audio-Visual Memory for On-Device Question Answering
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23085v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23085v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:09:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:09:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Qitong Wang, Jihui Jin, Divya Kothandaraman, Vishnu Raj, Christopher Rasmussen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Audio-visual question answering on lightweight user devices requires balancing speed, memory usage, privacy, and communication. One approach is to run audio-visual encoders on a server and a lightweight language decoder on the user device, keeping questions local while offloading media encoding. However, transferring and processing the full multimodal representation remains costly. We propose a training-free, query-agnostic token pruning framework that constructs a compact, reusable memory before transmission. We define video spatio-temporal blocks and aligned audio windows as players and estimate their importance through Monte Carlo Shapley valuation. We use an embedding coverage objective that combines query-independent utility with cross-modal redundancy calibration and rewards unimodal diversity through marginal coverage gains. We retain the original token embeddings of high-scoring players under a limited token budget and transfer the resulting memory once for reuse across subsequent local questions. Experiments on Video-MME, Daily-Omni, and Video-Holmes using video-SALMONN 2+ (3B) and video-SALMONN 2 Pro (4B) show that our method achieves the best or tied-best QA accuracy among the compared compression methods across all evaluated settings. On Daily-Omni with video-SALMONN 2+ (3B) at 80\% pruning, our method improves accuracy by 12.86--14.62 percentage points over these methods while achieving the lowest device-side LLM prefill and decoding latency, with up to $1.92\times$ decoding speedup at comparable embedding memory usage.



### Event-Based Person Detection for Maritime UAV Search
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23088v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23088v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:11:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:11:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Zijie Ning, Patrick Vandewalle
- **Comment**: Accepted at the 5th Workshop on Maritime Computer Vision (MaCVi), WACV 2027
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Maritime search and rescue requires locating people in the water quickly, motivating the use of UAVs to survey the search area. During flight, camera motion and the longer exposures used in low light can blur small targets and hinder person detection. Event cameras record brightness changes as they occur, reducing the motion blur associated with longer exposure times. We investigate their use for maritime person detection by generating synthetic events using the v2e event simulator from public MOBDrone rescue videos and retaining the existing annotations. We compare the recurrent vision transformer model RVT-T with YOLOv8n and RT-DETR-R18 trained on exposure-matched images. On 843 test samples from held-out recordings, RVT-T achieves 15.63% person AP50:95, compared with 6.89% for YOLOv8n and 9.44% for RT-DETR-R18 under 100 ms synthetic exposure. Increasing exposure times from 50 to 200 ms progressively reduces frame-based detection accuracy, widening the gap to the event model. To evaluate the feasibility of on-board processing, we deploy our detector on a Jetson Orin Nano platform. The RVT-T model retains its accuracy and requires approximately 136 ms and 2.02 J per sample, allowing real-time processing on the UAV.



### FMT: Lossless Image Compression in a Fixed Number of Parallel Steps
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23108v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23108v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:28:10+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:28:10+00:00
- **Authors**: Ammar Ali
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Learned lossless codecs make smaller files than common classical codecs, yet the images we archive and train on still use classical codecs. A main reason is time: a learned codec takes seconds to decode a photo that a classical codec opens in milliseconds, and their faster variants give up most of their gain in size. We argue that much of this time is spent waiting, not computing: the network waits for the values it conditions on, and the arithmetic decoder reads them one at a time. We introduce FMT (Fixed-step Multiscale Tree), a lossless image format whose decoding order and entropy coder are designed together so that neither wait grows with the image. FMT stores an image as a coarse-to-fine tree whose steps hold independent values: a GPU predicts each step at once and decodes it in parallel rANS lanes that carry their states from step to step, so an image of any size decodes in a fixed number of sequential steps. On this tree we build a family of bit-exact codecs, from a rule with no network to large networks, and no codec we ran on the same GPU beats any of them on both size and speed. Without a network, FMT beats NVIDIA's HTJ2K on size and speed; with a large one, it decodes Kodak photos 9 to 61X faster than learned codecs we ran on the same A100, at the same size or smaller.



### Event-Based Dynamic Lip Region Estimation During Speech
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23111v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23111v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:31:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:31:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Hamze Hammami, Omar Eldardeer, Francesco Rea
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Event cameras offer high temporal resolution and low power consumption, making them promising for speech-related applications. These include automatic lip reading, audiovisual speech analysis, and human-robot interaction, particularly in challenging environments characterized by low illumination or high dynamic range. Event-based lip reading, speaker recognition, and voice activity detection, however, mostly operate on a cropped region of interest (ROI), by hand or by the dataset, and no event-based method supplies that ROI as a spatial region; existing event-based face analysis gives the mouth as landmarks, which presents a mismatch between application and localization. The present work predicts a dense heatmap over the lip region from the full event "frame". Events are encoded as a polarity-separated voxel grid and processed at every location by a two-pathway continuous-time recurrent network (CT-RNN) with learned, log-space bounded time constants acting on the real elapsed time of each bin: a stateless fast pathway and a stateful slow pathway, utilised for refining and anchoring the prediction. The model reaches 69.5% on lips on four unseen speakers and switches target only 2.1 times per second, against 8.9 and 11.0 for convolutional baselines with   more parameters. Adding a spatial encoder improves precision but fails on a new face scale. On a real event sensor, trained only on simulated events, the proposed model reaches 58.0% on lips against 43.2% for the CNN, with fewer than half its target switches.



### LUTBench: Benchmarking Text-Driven Photographic Look Transfer
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23113v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23113v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:34:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:34:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Chuanzhi Xu, Boyu Wei, Chengkun Yue, Xuanhua Yin, Yiyang Li, Chupeng Liu, Zihan Deng, Weidong Cai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Photographic color grading shapes the tone and color of a photograph to achieve a desired look. Text-driven photographic look transfer lets users specify these adjustments in natural language, with the goal of reproducing the requested look across photographs while preserving their content. However, prior research on text-driven color grading uses different datasets, text instructions, target definitions, and metrics. These differences make it difficult to compare how faithfully the methods reproduce the same look. To compare methods under a shared protocol, we introduce LUTBench. We collect 91 three-dimensional lookup tables (3D LUTs) and define one photographic look per LUT with a specified input conversion. Applying these looks to 5,000 MIT-Adobe FiveK photographs produces 455,000 input-target image pairs with reproducible target images. For each look, we use templates to turn measured tone and color changes on fixed color samples into candidate clauses. We use training image measurements to verify applicable clauses before composing the text description. The evaluation measures target fidelity, tests sensitivity to predefined input conversions, and separates generalization to unseen looks from reproduction of seen looks on new photographs. Across 16 methods, stronger text alignment does not guarantee target fidelity. However, most supervised variants improve fidelity on both seen and unseen looks.



### Nunchi: Ears Help Eyes in Multi-Party Conversations
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23115v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23115v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:35:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:35:07+00:00
- **Authors**: JunYeong Park, Shijing Wang, Seonwook Park, Michael Stengel, Shalini De Mello, Sang-Il Choi, Hyung Jin Chang
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Identifying whom a person looks at is central to understanding multi-party conversations, yet gaze geometry alone becomes ambiguous as more people appear. Conversations offer cues that geometry lacks, such as who is speaking and what is said, but no gaze dataset provides them for many multi-party scenes. We introduce SORBET, a benchmark of 3,340 samples from 1,578 clips with synchronized speech, transcripts, and attended person labels, covering more multi-party scenes than existing gaze datasets. Prior gaze models struggle to use these cues, which come in different forms, must be linked to the right person, and can conflict with gaze geometry. We propose Nunchi, a framework that constructs a text prompt from the transcript and the outputs of gaze and speaker models, links every cue to the candidates with shared labels, and lets a vision-language model (VLM) weigh gaze geometry against conversational cues to identify the attended person. Nunchi outperforms the strongest existing method by 4.8 percentage points. On SORBET, conversational cues help whereas additional visual cues do not, and the gains hold across three VLMs, with automatic transcripts, and on two existing datasets.



### The Illusion of Grounding: Do Vision-Language Models Weigh Retrieved Text and Images Like an Ideal Observer?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23127v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CL, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23127v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:45:28+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:45:28+00:00
- **Authors**: Basavaraj R M, Harshal Chandel
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 5 figures, 11 tables. Code and data: https://github.com/Basavarajearlybird/GIB-200K (archived at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24320174)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Retrieval-augmented vision-language models (VLMs) answer from two sources of evidence that can each fail: the retrieved text can be wrong or noisy, and the image can be degraded or contradicted. We introduce GIB-200K, 200,000 image-question items built from COCO and Visual Genome, each expanded into controlled stress conditions: hard-negative retrieval noise (1-20 documents), visual ambiguity (Gaussian and motion blur, low-resource crops) and cross-modal conflict with counterfactual flip pairs. Instead of only measuring degradation, we ask how models combine the two modalities. From ideal Bayesian cue combination we derive a testable prediction that yields formal definitions of Knowledge Inertia (text over-weighted relative to the model's own image evidence) and Sensory Capture (the reverse), and we estimate the weights with a measurement-error-corrected regression whose recovery of known weights we verify. A counterfactual flip decomposition separates modality bias from concept priors, and a Grounding Illusion Score measures how much clean accuracy is not grounded in the image. We evaluate 4 open models on one frozen, seeded evaluation split of 2,000 GIB-200K items. All models are accurate on clean inputs (92.5%-94.2%) but adverse accuracy falls to 41.3%-59.9%. The two LLaVA models over-weight the text (Knowledge Inertia, w_T/w_I = 1.229 and 1.322), whereas InternVL3 favours the image (w_T/w_I = 0.680); between 34.8% and 71.4% of the clean answers that are correct are abandoned when the document lies. A training-free consistency check that compares the answer with and without retrieval trades coverage for accuracy under misleading documents, while falling back to the image fails when the image is heavily degraded. All per-instance outputs, code, and an audit that recomputes every reported open-model number are released.



### GameRealm: Grounding Visual Learning in Interactive Games
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23140v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23140v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:57:22+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:57:22+00:00
- **Authors**: Weiguang Zhao, Yifan Wang, Ni Yang, Yating Wang, Xingyu Miao, Yankai Jiang, Mulin Yu, Tao Lu, Haoyu Guo, Junting Dong, Weinan Zhang, Chunhua Shen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual foundation models are central to physical intelligence, yet their development remains constrained by fragmented training datasets, inconsistent annotation quality, and limited multimodal supervision. To address these challenges, we introduce GameRealm, a large-scale, richly annotated dataset that grounds visual observations in synchronized, game-native supervision. GameRealm encompasses over 120 million RGB frames and 1,000 hours of gameplay footage across all views. Beyond its unprecedented scale, GameRealm provides rich multimodal supervision, including metric depth, camera parameters, synchronized audio, control inputs, and 3D point trajectories. Its diversity further extends to flexible view configurations, spanning single-view, stereo, multi-view, and panoramic recordings. To enable scalable acquisition across diverse game environments, we develop a unified data collection pipeline that combines human-operated interaction with agentic exploration. Extensive experiments across diverse visual learning tasks demonstrate that GameRealm consistently strengthens visual foundation models in both visual generation and geometric learning. These results establish GameRealm as a versatile and valuable data resource, unlocking new opportunities for advancing visual intelligence.



### TCP4D: A Unified Tool-Center-Point Tracking and Generation Model for Robot Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23143v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23143v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:59:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:59:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Haosong Peng, Xiangyang Liu, Zhengshen Zhang, Yunfeng Fan, Tianyu Qi, Xin Xia, Yufeng Zhan, Wenchao Xu
- **Comment**: Project page: https://livioni.github.io/TCP4D/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robot manipulation videos provide a rich visual experience, yet recovering robot action and gripper-state annotations from RGB observations still remains challenging. Using the embodiment-agnostic tool-center-point (TCP) pose as the action space, we present TCP4D, a unified model in which a shared 4D scene representation both tracks observed TCP motion and generates future TCP trajectories. Given a monocular video and initial 2D TCP queries, the tracker directly recovers metric TCP poses in SE(3) and gripper states alongside scene geometry and camera parameters. The generator samples local features from the same 4D representation along the recovered TCP tracks, then combines them with robot observations and language instructions to predict future TCP states. Our new cross-embodiment augmentation pipeline converts existing RGB videos into demonstrations for alternative embodiments by retargeting the recovered motion and synthesizing the corresponding observations, without recorded source-robot states. Experiments show that the tracker achieves absolute trajectory errors of 7.60 and 16.07 mm on DROID and RoboLab and adapts to a new embodiment with only ten demonstrations. The generator achieves 69.19% mean success on RoboTwin and outperforms well-established policy models on two real-world tasks, while joint training on source and augmented demonstrations improves target-robot success by 41.5% over augmented-only training.



### $π^4$: A Foundation Model for 4D Scene Geometry and Motion
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23144v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23144v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 17:59:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 17:59:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Yifan Wang, Weiguang Zhao, Ni Yang, Haoyu Guo, Junting Dong, Weinan Zhang, Chunhua Shen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: $π^4$ is a unified foundation model for 4D scene perception. From diverse, uncalibrated RGB observations in the wild---a single image, an unordered collection, or a video sequence---it recovers dense geometry, camera poses, and metric scale, together with 2D/3D point tracks and moving-object masks for multi-image inputs. It couples a geometry branch with a geometry-conditioned motion branch, which predicts image-space correspondences that are lifted into 3D using reconstructed target-view geometry. Across diverse benchmarks, $π^4$ achieves the best overall average rank in geometry evaluation, alongside competitive performance in single- and multiview 2D/3D point tracking and moving-object segmentation. With a shared backbone, the full geometry-and-motion system contains approximately 0.5B parameters---less than half the size of most baselines. It produces joint reconstructions and tracks in one forward pass, including large-scale scenes with thousands of input images despite training with contexts of at most 64 views. We further present the data and training recipe underlying these capabilities, together with empirical studies of key design choices. Code and models are available at https://github.com/yyfz/Pi4.



### VIVID: Beyond Static Multi-Image Reasoning with Localized Visual Differences and Textual Entity Dynamics in Video MLLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23153v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23153v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 18:05:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 18:05:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Chengqian Ma, Yuanze Zeng, Hongyu Wang, Mingfeng Lin, YiCheng Xiao, Yu Chen, Pengyu Li, WenHao Wang, Yuexiao Ma, Xiawu Zheng, Fei Chao
- **Comment**: 14 pages, 2 figures, 5 tables. Code: https://github.com/step-out/VIVID
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video question answering under tight frame budgets requires preserving localized motion and event order, yet current video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) reduce comprehension to static multi-image reasoning by discarding unselected frames and encoding keyframes independently: (1) Spatially, whole-frame pooling dilutes small moving targets into static backgrounds; (2) Temporally, dropping intermediate frames or averaging tokens erases inter-frame motion, confusing direction, speed, and order. We propose VIVID (Visual Inter-frame Variations and Individual Dynamics), which moves beyond static snapshots like human vision: keyframes capture core states while inter-frame Patch Diffs preserve neighboring changes within $K_f$ keyframes. Autonomous Delta-Driven Keyframe Planning (ADKP) selects anchors from a $3\text{ fps}$ pool and feeds surrounding patch differences into Dual-Road Change Memory (DRCM), forming Dynamic Tokens at zero extra anchor tokens alongside a camera-stabilized Active Motion Region of Interest (ROI) and chronological Entity State Chains. Adding $0.22\%$ trainable parameters to a frozen $27\text{B}$ backbone, VIVID-Small ($50$ examples, $\sim 1.5$ GPU hours) beats all eleven baselines in overall accuracy across $8,083$ questions and trails only VIVID-Large ($3,600$ examples), which leads on MotionBench, Video-MME, and TempCompass.



### TextSphere: Efficient Text-to-Image Generation via Shared Hyperspherical Alignment and Uniformity
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23155v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23155v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 18:08:53+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 18:08:53+00:00
- **Authors**: Yixia Zhang, Girish A. Koushik, Xilu Wang, Wenwu Wang
- **Comment**: 21 pages, 8 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent advances in text-to-image generation have substantially improved image quality, particularly through diffusion-based methods that require multiple inference steps. In contrast, Sphere Encoder offers an efficient generative framework capable of producing images with fewer forward steps. However, extending it to free-form text is nontrivial, because its class-conditioned design lacks cross-modal alignment. To address this challenge, we propose TextSphere, an efficient text-to-image generative framework that extends Sphere Encoder with language conditioning and cross-modal alignment. We incorporate pretrained CLIP text embeddings through adaptive layer normalization (AdaLN) to guide image generation while retaining the original spherical sampling mechanism. To bridge the cross-modal representation gap, we introduce a Spherical Semantic Alignment and Uniformity (SSAU) objective that jointly promotes semantic alignment and representation uniformity on the hypersphere. Experiments on CUB-200-2011, MS-COCO-2014, and Flickr30k demonstrate that TextSphere consistently reduces generation FID (gFID) over the text-conditioned baseline while achieving reconstruction FID (rFID) below 1.0. With only 4 and 6 sampling steps, TextSphere achieves lower gFID than the compared diffusion- and GAN-based models trained from scratch on CUB-200-2011 and MS-COCO-2014, respectively. We further observe that improved reconstruction fidelity does not necessarily yield better generation quality. In addition, we use Partial Least Squares Singular Value Decomposition (PLS-SVD) to analyze cross-modal alignment and modality gap, offering insight into the effectiveness of our approach.



### Attribution Through the Decoder: Prediction-Space Explanations for Structured Outputs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23161v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23161v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 18:13:50+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 18:13:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Zaer Bin Zaqy, Brinnae Bent
- **Comment**: Under review
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Networks that predict angles, gaze directions, and rotations do not output these quantities directly: they emit raw coordinates that a fixed decoder, such as normalization, maps to the final prediction. Attribution methods are usually applied to the raw coordinates, so they can credit inputs for changes the decoder discards, such as rescaling a vector when only its direction matters. The resulting explanation can highlight inputs that have no effect on the prediction. We instead attribute through the decoder differential, which for normalization decoders reduces to a scaled tangent projection. We characterize invariance to output rescaling, show that every fixed smooth scalar target on a sphere has points of zero tangential sensitivity, and show that an endpoint-projected variant of Integrated Gradients can still attribute to scale-only inputs, whereas direct angular Integrated Gradients cannot. On synthetic tasks with analytically known relevance, ambient attribution assigns up to 72% of its mass to an irrelevant region. On MPIIGaze validation data, tangent Grad-CAM produces 44% greater prediction displacement under 10% pixel deletion. On PASCAL3D+ with a 6D rotation decoder, the full differential improves mean deletion displacement by 14.1 degrees over the ambient coordinate sum and by 13.8 degrees over a decoder-invariant scalar. Results for a two-polarity tangent LayerCAM variant are mixed: it exceeds ambient-sum LayerCAM on circular regression but no difference is detected on quaternion pose. These results separate two failure sources, radial contamination and scalarization loss. The approach uses existing attribution methods with decoder-aware targets, although some variants also require changes to attribution aggregation. An invariant attribution can still miss inputs that drive the prediction, so invariance and deletion performance should each be checked.



### Déjà View: A Shared Working-Memory Model for Visual Tasks
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23177v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG, q-bio.NC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23177v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 18:23:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 18:23:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Mranmay Shetty, Gabriel Kreiman
- **Comment**: 6 figures; includes appendices
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A working-memory model must remember stimuli and use its memory flexibly to perform different tasks. Here we introduce a model of working memory for images that combines dynamic activity with temporary associations between image features. We train the model on five tasks, including remembering a sample while other images are presented, categorizing distractor images while remembering the sample, and determining whether an image is new or has been presented before. The same trained model performs all tasks, guided by explicit cues about which response is required. We use this flexible memory model to ask how delay, distraction, and image history affect performance. Delays have little effect on sample-memory performance for the delays we tested, while seeing more images leads to more false alarms even when the target image appeared equally recently in all cases. On the sample-memory-plus-categorization task, the probability of correct responses on all questions decreases as more questions are asked, despite robust sample-memory performance. Finally, the model maintains stable performance across 40 brief trials of each task under the tested conditions. Together, these results demonstrate how a single working-memory system can be flexibly used to perform different tasks, and how its errors depend on both previous images and task demands.



### Say Less, Hallucinate Less? Diagnosing and Restoring Object Coverage in Preference-Optimized Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23179v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23179v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-17 18:24:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-17 18:24:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Hansang Lee
- **Comment**: 18 pages, 6 figures, 19 tables. Main text and appendices
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Preference optimization (PO) is the standard way to reduce object hallucination in large vision-language models. We find that four public PO checkpoints of LLaVA-1.5-7B also lower object coverage. They name fewer object categories, and their lower hallucination rates bring no significant gain in set-level object F1 on CHAIR.
